## Added notes

### Note 1784249805826
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "{{c1::30–70}}<strong>% of RLNs</strong> demonstrate extralaryngeal branching, with {{c2::bifurcation::type of branching}}&nbsp;accounting for the large majority. If a {{c2::bifurcation}} occurs, the {{c3::anterior::anterior/posterior}} branch is typically the motor branch."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Photo below is a little misleading. The branching usually occurs within 1cm of the entry point<br>The branching discussed above refers to the nerve fibers that enter the larynx. There are other extralaryngeal branches off of the RLN that innervate the esophagus and trachea, especially on the left side.<br><br>Remember that the RLN is part of the vagus nerve that forms form the 6th branchial arch. In addition to controlling the vocal cords, the vagus nerve helps with volitional swallow so it makes sense that it also innervates the esophagus.<br><br><img src=\"paste-f591c6c95ef206c71496fbadabe0d3dd82de46ca.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "mTD7Y+;&],",
  "id": 1784249805826,
  "media": [
    "paste-f591c6c95ef206c71496fbadabe0d3dd82de46ca.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Procedures::Thyroidectomy",
    "Crabs::Residency::HN::Thyroidectomy"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1784249919504
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The RLN typically courses {{c1::posteriorly}} to the inferior thyroid artery"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<ul>\n\n\n\n<li>The RLN is usually posterior to the ITA but can also be anterior to it or between ITA branches</li><li>More predictable on the patient's left side. More variability on the right side</li></ul><img src=\"paste-5de30eb4553652f8e992670ad211148837ad3838.jpg\"><br>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "yI<eU,|d_`",
  "id": 1784249919504,
  "media": [
    "paste-5de30eb4553652f8e992670ad211148837ad3838.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Procedures::Thyroidectomy",
    "Crabs::Residency::HN::Thyroidectomy"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1784250575341
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "<div>Most commonly, the RLN runs {{c1::posterior/deep::relationship}}<b>&nbsp;to Berry's ligament</b>.</div>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div><div>Can also</div><div>* Run&nbsp;<b>between fibers or branches of the ligament</b>.</div><div>* Rarely run&nbsp;<b>anterior/superficial to part of the ligament</b>.</div></div><div><br></div><div><img src=\"paste-b2ca6e9236ea7c331c20b78e56fda01024a3a70c.jpg\"></div>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "O1l$!#OmWX",
  "id": 1784250575341,

}
```

### Note 1784250784382
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "RLN normally sits {{c1::posteriormedial}} to the tubercle of Zuckerkandl."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "About 70% of people have a tubercle. Nerve can also run lateral to or on top of it.&nbsp;<br><img src=\"paste-119da77029e4aa992bd5370e6c6cb9517d864ab5.jpg\"><br>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "tP6rvi[zD>",

  ],

  ]
}
```

### Note 1784250932732
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The {{c1::Cernea}} classification is used to the describe the path of the {{c2::external branch of the superior laryngeal nerve}} as it enters the larynx"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Type 1: Crosses the superior thyroid vessels &gt;1 cm above the superior pole → lowest risk, ~40–60% <br>Type 2a: Crosses the vessels &lt;1 cm above the superior pole → intermediate risk, ~20–30% - more common with large goiters <br>Type 2b: Crosses the vessels at or below the superior pole → highest risk during superior pole dissection, ~10–25%<br><img src=\"paste-b62d69f01cdf22d8dae5648ee7b121a579591041.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "pzQ7XYPg/c",
  "id": 1784250932732,
  "media": [
    "paste-b62d69f01cdf22d8dae5648ee7b121a579591041.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Procedures::Thyroidectomy",
    "Crabs::Residency::HN::Thyroidectomy"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1786014226644
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "What flap dis?<br>{{c1::Rieger flap<br>}}<br><img alt=\"Expanded Applications of the Dorsal Nasal Flap\" src=\"images-bc7deb287086e158de0d639d5a1654e1b27ba145.jpg\">"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<li>AKA dorsal nasal flap</li><li><strong>Rotation flap</strong> recruiting skin from the <strong>glabella and upper nasal dorsum</strong>\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Best for:</strong> medium (<strong>~1.5–2.5 cm</strong>) defects of the <strong>distal dorsum, supratip, and nasal tip</strong>\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Advantages:</strong> single stage, excellent color/texture match, reliable random-pattern blood supply\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Limitations:</strong> requires adequate glabellar laxity; may cause tip elevation or alar distortion in large defects\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Not ideal for:</strong> large (&gt;2.5–3 cm), full-thickness, or major alar defects (consider paramedian forehead flap)</li>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "CfvL2?gNNG",
  "id": 1786014226644,
  "media": [
    "images-bc7deb287086e158de0d639d5a1654e1b27ba145.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::FacialPlastics::LocalFlaps:Rotation:DorsalNasal",
    "Crabs::Residency::FacialPlastics::MohsRepair::Nose"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1786931546055
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "What flap is this?<br>{{c1::Note Flap}}<br><img src=\"paste-740f023f6b351f8285c05c0d956d84601cea5c4b.jpg\">"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div>The note flap described by Walike and Larrabee is an</div>\n<div>angular transposition flap that, when designed, looks like</div>\n<div>a musical eighth note.5 This flap allows one to close a</div>\n<div>circular defect with a triangular transposition flap that</div>\n<div>maximizes the use of surrounding tissue (Fig. 8-3).6 The</div>\n<div>flap is designed by first drawing a tangent on either side</div>\n<div>of the circle parallel to relaxed skin tension lines (RSTLs).</div>\n<div>The tangent should have the length of 1.5 times the</div>\n<div>diameter of the circular defect. At the end of the tangent</div>\n<div>line, a 50° to 60° angled flap is designed, with the second</div>\n<div>side of the flap having a length approximately equal to</div>\n<div>the diameter of the circle.6 The flap is transposed into</div>\n<div>the defect, and the distal tip of the flap is trimmed so that</div>\n<div>there is no wound closure tension. As with rectangular</div>\n<div>and parabolic transposition flaps, the greatest wound</div>\n<div>closure tension is at the closure of the donor site. In the</div>\n<div>case of the note flap, the greatest wound closure tension</div>\n<div>is approximately perpendicular to the tangent line created</div>\n<div>when the flap is designed. The surface area of the note</div>\n<div>flap is designed so that it is 25% less than the area of the</div>\n<div>defect; thus clinical judgment is required when this flap</div>\n<div>is used. The flap is recommended primarily for small</div>\n<div>(2 cm or less) skin defects. Because the pivotal arc of the</div>\n<div>note flap is approximately 45°, a minimal standing cutaneous</div>\n<div>deformity develops that may not require excision.</div>\n<div>Standing cutaneous deformities of larger flaps will require</div>\n<div>subsequent surgical revision unless the deformities are</div>\n<div>removed at the time of flap transfer. I use the note flap</div>\n<div>primarily for defects on the cheek, temple, and lateral</div>\n<div>nasal sidewall. However, it may be used everywhere on</div>\n<div>the head and neck, including the lip.</div>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "QO%8,!L_(D",
  "id": 1786931546055,
  "media": [
    "paste-740f023f6b351f8285c05c0d956d84601cea5c4b.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::FacialPlastics::LocalFlaps::Transposition"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1786973790266
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "<div><div><div><div><strong>Dorsal nasal flap (Rieger flap):</strong>&nbsp;modified {{c1::rotation}} recruiting redundant skin from the {{c1::glabella}} → repairs defects of the {{c1::nasal tip, dorsum, and sidewall}} up to {{c1::~2.5 cm}} → axial blood supply from a branch of the {{c1::angular artery}} with pedicle centered near the {{c1::medial canthus}} → major disadvantage is {{c1::cephalic displacement of the nostril margin and nasal tip}}.</div></div><div></div><div><div></div></div></div><div><div></div></div></div><div></div>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<img src=\"paste-a2562d67b26b2ff62184898c2d31da7329baecc7.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "Jgm[z1Cd~=",
  "id": 1786973790266,
  "media": [
    "paste-a2562d67b26b2ff62184898c2d31da7329baecc7.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::FacialPlastics::LocalFlaps:Rotation:DorsalNasal"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1786974024413
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "What flap is this?<br>{{c1::Bilobe}}<br><img src=\"paste-7aed66bd5ae1b6dc3a67993e00b2b5c98365a2ad.jpg\">"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<li><strong>Type:</strong> Double transposition flap.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Uses:</strong> Small-to-moderate defects of the distal/lower third of the nose, particularly the nasal tip and ala; typically defects ≤1.5 cm.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Design:</strong> Two sequential lobes. The first lobe is approximately the size of the defect; the second is smaller and helps close the first-lobe donor site. The Zitelli modification uses a total rotation arc of ~90–110°, with ~45–50° between each lobe.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Disadvantages:</strong> Trapdoor/pincushion deformity, standing cutaneous deformity, relatively long scars, and alar retraction/elevation from excessive tension.</li>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "vH$$*w3|W:",
  "id": 1786974024413,
  "media": [
    "paste-7aed66bd5ae1b6dc3a67993e00b2b5c98365a2ad.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::FacialPlastics::LocalFlaps::Bilobe"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1786974160978
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "<strong>Bilobed flap:</strong> {{c1::double transposition::type}} flap&nbsp;→ used for {{c1::small-to-moderate distal nasal defects, especially the tip and ala::locations}} typically {{c1::≤1.5::size}} cm→ first lobe is {{c1::~equal}}&nbsp;to defect size, second lobe is {{c1::smaller}}; Zitelli design uses a {{c1::~90–110° total rotation arc (~45–50° per lobe::rotation)}} → disadvantages include {{c1::trapdoor/pincushion deformity, standing cutaneous deformity, long scars, and alar retraction/elevation from excessive tension}}."
```

### Note 1786989102446
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "<strong>Rhombic flap design:</strong> Greatest closure tension occurs at the {{c1::donor}} site, ~20° from the short diagonal across the flap base. Wide undermining has {{c1::minimal}} effect on closure tension. Major disadvantage is that it {{c1::crosses multiple RSTL}}"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<li>Tension is on the donor side because it is a transposition flap. TransDonor</li><li><img src=\"paste-d9120f44718c51e721d56497797601dc00e0a8c2.jpg\"></li>"
```

### Note 1788280963413
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "<div>Hearing aids produce clinically meaningful tinnitus improvement in approximately {{c1::~2/3::proportion}} of patients with hearing loss</div>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<ul><li>“Clinically meaningful” should mean a&nbsp;<strong>patient-perceived meaningful reduction in tinnitus burden</strong>, commonly operationalized using a TFI reduction of about&nbsp;<strong>≥13 points</strong>.</li><li>The original TFI validation proposed&nbsp;<strong>13 points</strong>&nbsp;as the threshold for meaningful change.</li><li>Benefit is primarily decreased&nbsp;<strong>bothersomeness/functional impact</strong>, not necessarily elimination of tinnitus.</li><li>Dedicated tinnitus masker/sound-generator features have&nbsp;<strong>not consistently outperformed amplification alone</strong>.</li></ul>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "gLx5~;6Dl@",
  "id": 1788280963413,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::AssistiveHearingDevicesHearingAids",
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::SubjectiveTinnitus"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788281027021
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "<div>Tinnitus Functional Index (TFI) score &gt;{{c1::65}} indicates <strong>high/severe tinnitus impact</strong>.</div>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div><ul><li><strong>25 items</strong>, scored&nbsp;<strong>0–100</strong>; higher = worse tinnitus-related functional impact.</li><li>There&nbsp;<strong>are proposed severity categories</strong>, although they're not as universally standardized as the scale itself.</li><li>A large 1,114-patient study proposed four grades:&nbsp;<strong>low, lower-moderate, upper-moderate, and high</strong>, with&nbsp;<strong>&gt;65 = high severity</strong>.</li><li>The literature also contains alternative grading systems, so I would&nbsp;<strong>not memorize rigid “mild/moderate/severe” cutoffs</strong>&nbsp;as though they're universally accepted.</li><li><strong>Meaningful improvement:</strong>&nbsp;traditionally&nbsp;<strong>↓ ≥13 points</strong>.</li><li>Newer 2025 anchor-based data estimated an MCID closer to&nbsp;<strong>~9 points</strong>, suggesting the exact threshold depends somewhat on baseline severity and follow-up interval.</li></ul><div>For boards/clinical use, I'd remember&nbsp;<strong>TFI 0–100; &gt;65 = high impact; ~13-point decrease = traditional clinically meaningful improvement</strong>.</div></div>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "v>=T&^p#@*",
  "id": 1788281027021,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::General::PROMs",
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::AssistiveHearingDevicesHearingAids",
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::SubjectiveTinnitus"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788281621253
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "<div>A clinically meaningful improvement in the Dizziness Handicap Inventory (DHI) is a decrease of ≥{{c1::18 points}}.</div>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div>DHI is a 25-item (0–100) patient-reported measure of dizziness-related handicap. Yes = 4, Sometimes = 2, No = 0. Higher scores = greater handicap. Common severity classification:&nbsp;<strong>0–30 mild, 31–60 moderate, 61–100 severe</strong>. Subscales are physical, functional, and emotional.<b></b></div><div><br></div><div>Score &gt;60 correlates with high specificity to presence of comorbid functional or psychitric conditions<br>Score &lt;30 correlates to abscence of comorbid condition</div>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "q|X+*POgn#",
  "id": 1788281621253,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::General::PROMs",
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::PeripheralVestibularDisorders",
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::VestibularTesting"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788303820688
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Unlike a modified radical/CWD mastoidectomy, a radical mastoidectomy sacrifices the {{c1::TM and ossicular hearing mechanism}} and exteriorizes the {{c1::middle ear + mastoid}} into a common cavity."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div>The defining idea is <strong>sacrifice of the conductive hearing apparatus for maximal disease eradication</strong>.</div>\n<ul>\n<li>\nPosterior canal wall is removed.\n</li>\n<li>\nTM is removed/not reconstructed.\n</li>\n<li>\nMalleus/incus are removed; middle-ear contents are cleared as required for disease.\n</li>\n<li>\nMiddle ear, attic, and mastoid become a single exteriorized cavity.\n</li>\n<li>\nWide meatoplasty allows surveillance and cleaning.</li></ul><div><table><tbody><tr><th></th><th>CWU</th><th>CWD / modified radical</th><th>Radical</th></tr><tr><td>Posterior canal wall</td><td>Preserved</td><td>Removed</td><td>Removed</td></tr><tr><td>Mastoid exteriorized into EAC</td><td>No</td><td>Yes</td><td>Yes</td></tr><tr><td>TM</td><td>Preserved/reconstructed</td><td>Preserved/reconstructed</td><td>Sacrificed</td></tr><tr><td>Hearing mechanism</td><td>Preserve/reconstruct</td><td>Preserve/reconstruct</td><td>Sacrificed</td></tr><tr><td>Middle ear</td><td>Functional space maintained</td><td>Functional space maintained if possible</td><td>Exteriorized</td></tr><tr><td>Priority</td><td>Disease control + anatomy</td><td>Disease control + hearing when possible</td><td>Disease eradication</td></tr></tbody></table></div>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "vLq#mHi-Yv",
  "id": 1788303820688,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Mastoidectomy"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788304334927
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Pulsatile tinnitus<br>High-pitched/“pulsing” PT + pulsatile bruit + <strong>no change with ipsilateral jugular compression</strong> → consider {{c1::arterial causes}} First line work up after exam {{c3::CTA}}"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div>Examples:</div>\n<ul>\n<li>\nCarotid atherosclerotic stenosis\n</li>\n<li>\nCarotid dissection\n</li>\n<li>\nFibromuscular dysplasia\n</li>\n<li>\nTortuous ICA\n</li>\n<li>\nAberrant ICA\n</li>\n<li>\nAneurysm\n</li>\n</ul>\n<div><strong>Pearl:</strong> Sudden-onset PT + neck pain/headache or neurologic symptoms → consider <strong>carotid dissection</strong>.</div>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "NP,8BJB=*4",
  "id": 1788304334927,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::PulsatileTinnitus"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788304354597
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Pulsatile tinnitus<br><div>Objective PT/bruit without typical venous features should raise concern for an {{c1::AV shunting lesion, particularly a dAVF}}. If genuinely concerned this is the dx, work up after exam with {{c2::neurovascular eval +/- DSA}}</div>\n<div></div>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<li>Can't miss dx</li><li><br></li><li>Bruit is basically the breakpoint for considering an AVM. Could be arterial but you wont know until you get imaging.</li><li><br></li><li><strong>dAVF</strong>&nbsp;= dural arteriio venous fistula is the important AV shunt in isolated PT.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>AVM:</strong> artery → nidus → vein.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>dAVF:</strong> meningeal artery → dural vein/sinus without a nidus.\n</li>\n<li>\ndAVF may present with <strong>PT alone</strong>.\n</li>\n<li>\nDangerous dAVFs have <strong>cortical venous reflux</strong>, which increases risk of intracranial hemorrhage.\n</li>\n<li>\nAVM becomes more suspicious with associated <strong>seizure, focal neurologic symptoms, or intracranial hemorrhage</strong>.</li>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "h3)sC8^,0%",
  "id": 1788304354597,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::PulsatileTinnitus"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788304372470
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Pulsatile tinnitus<br>PT that changes/disappears with {{c1::ipsilateral jugular compression or head position}} suggests a {{c2::venous source}}. First line work up after exam {{c3::CTA}}"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div>Think along the venous pathway:</div>\n<div><strong>Transverse sinus → sigmoid sinus → jugular bulb</strong></div>\n<div>Examples:</div>\n<ul>\n<li>\nVenous sinus stenosis\n</li>\n<li>\nSigmoid sinus diverticulum\n</li>\n<li>\nSigmoid sinus dehiscence\n</li>\n<li>\nHigh-riding/dehiscent jugular bulb\n</li>\n<li>\nJugular bulb diverticulum\n</li>\n<li>\nEnlarged emissary veins\n</li>\n</ul>\n<div><strong>Pearl:</strong> Dehiscence may allow otherwise normal venous flow to become audible because bone no longer insulates the middle/inner ear from vascular sound.</div>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "b>q?hJ7Uhk",
  "id": 1788304372470,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::PulsatileTinnitus"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788304391144
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Pulsatile tinnitus<br><div>PT + {{c1::headache, visual symptoms and/or papilledema}} → consider {{c2::idiopathic intracranial hypertension (IIH)}}. After exam work up with {{c3::MRI/MRV + eye exam}}</div>\n<div></div>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div><strong>Extra:</strong><br>\nClassic association: younger woman with elevated BMI.</div>\n<div>Ask about:</div>\n<ul>\n<li>\nHeadache\n</li>\n<li>\nTransient visual obscurations\n</li>\n<li>\nDiplopia\n</li>\n<li>\nOther visual changes\n</li>\n</ul>\n<div>PT in IIH is strongly associated with <strong>transverse venous sinus stenosis</strong>.</div>\n<div>Evaluation may include:</div>\n<ul>\n<li>\nOphthalmologic exam for papilledema\n</li>\n<li>\nMRI/MRV\n</li>\n<li>\nLP with opening pressure when appropriate</li></ul>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "Er)X4TjFe3",
  "id": 1788304391144,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::PulsatileTinnitus"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788304412811
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Pulsatile tinnitus<br><div><div>PT + a {{c1::red/purple pulsatile retrotympanic mass}} → consider {{c2::paraganglioma or a vascular anomaly}}. Work up after exam with {{c3::CT temporal bone}}</div>\n<div></div></div>\n<div></div>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div>Differential:</div>\n<ul>\n<li>\nGlomus tympanicum\n</li>\n<li>\nGlomus jugulare\n</li>\n<li>\nHigh/dehiscent jugular bulb\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Aberrant ICA</strong>\n</li>\n</ul>\n<div><strong>Pearl:</strong> Don't assume a vascular-appearing middle-ear mass is a tumor—an <strong>aberrant ICA can mimic one</strong>.</div>\n<div>High-resolution <strong>CT temporal bone</strong> is particularly useful for defining the bony/vascular anatomy.</div>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "v~75$[ADe`",
  "id": 1788304412811,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::PulsatileTinnitus"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788311829264
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Attic retraction<br>Tos {{c1::I}}<br><img src=\"paste-8f0a746abbc463a6dc01b48516973fa6cbe498c2.jpg\"><br>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Tos/Poulsen — pars flaccida:<br>\nI = toward malleus neck, <strong>air space remains</strong><br>\nII = <strong>contacts malleus neck</strong>, no air space<br>\nIII = extends beyond malleus, <strong>fundus/full extent visible</strong>, limited scutal erosion<br>\nIV = <strong>severe scutal/outer attic wall erosion<br></strong><br><li><strong>Can I see the fundus?</strong> If you can't see the bottom of the pocket, you can't exclude retained keratin deeper in the attic.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Is it self-cleaning, or is it retaining keratin/debris?</strong> Retained keratin is the major red flag for cholesteatoma.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Is there scutal/bony erosion?</strong> Erosion suggests more advanced/destructive disease.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Is it progressing?</strong> A stable shallow pocket is very different from one getting deeper over serial exams.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Are the ossicles involved / is there conductive hearing loss?</strong></li><br>There is a new classification system that adds additional categories<br><a href=\"https://drive.google.com/file/d/17XHUEyEC7LzsQDsVXPwDqp9Wo526fz9x/view?usp=sharing\">https://drive.google.com/file/d/17XHUEyEC7LzsQDsVXPwDqp9Wo526fz9x/view?usp=sharing</a><br>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "r9266CL38U",
  "id": 1788311829264,
  "media": [
    "paste-8f0a746abbc463a6dc01b48516973fa6cbe498c2.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Cholesteatoma"
  ]
}
```

```json
      "value": "Tos/Poulsen — pars flaccida:<br>\nI = toward malleus neck, <strong>air space remains</strong><br>\nII = <strong>contacts malleus neck</strong>, no air space<br>\nIII = extends beyond malleus, <strong>fundus/full extent visible</strong>, limited scutal erosion<br>\nIV = <strong>severe scutal/outer attic wall erosion<br></strong><br><li><strong>Can I see the fundus?</strong> If you can't see the bottom of the pocket, you can't exclude retained keratin deeper in the attic.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Is it self-cleaning, or is it retaining keratin/debris?</strong> Retained keratin is the major red flag for cholesteatoma.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Is there scutal/bony erosion?</strong> Erosion suggests more advanced/destructive disease.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Is it progressing?</strong> A stable shallow pocket is very different from one getting deeper over serial exams.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Are the ossicles involved / is there conductive hearing loss?</strong></li><br>There is a new classification system that adds additional categories<br><a href=\"https://drive.google.com/file/d/17XHUEyEC7LzsQDsVXPwDqp9Wo526fz9x/view?usp=sharing\">https://drive.google.com/file/d/17XHUEyEC7LzsQDsVXPwDqp9Wo526fz9x/view?usp=sharing</a><br>"
```

```json
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div><strong>Sade classification — pars tensa retraction</strong></div>\n<ul>\n<li>\n<strong>I:</strong> Mild retraction\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>II:</strong> TM contacts <strong>incus/stapes</strong>\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>III:</strong> TM contacts <strong>promontory</strong>\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>IV:</strong> TM <strong>adherent to promontory</strong> → adhesive otitis\n</li>\n</ul>\n<div><strong>Clinical significance</strong></div>\n<ul>\n<li>\n<strong>Ossicular contact matters:</strong> chronic retraction can erode the <strong>long process of the incus</strong> → ossicular discontinuity and CHL, even without cholesteatoma.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>III → IV:</strong> distinguishes <strong>promontory contact</strong> from actual <strong>adhesion</strong>; IV represents established adhesive middle-ear disease.\n</li>\n<li>\nThe <strong>grade alone does not determine treatment</strong>. More important are <strong>hearing loss, ossicular erosion, keratin trapping/cholesteatoma, ability to completely visualize the pocket, and progression over time</strong>.</li></ul>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "wU}.)%@,4j",
  "id": 1788312407162,
  "media": [
    "paste-534dbc2bd3a9c57ad212eaff742f18099e03ae00.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Cholesteatoma"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788347833375
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Best behavioral audiology modality by age: ; {{c1::6mo-2y}} = {{c2::visual reinforcement audiometry::test}};"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<table><tbody><tr><th>Approx. developmental age</th><th>Test</th><th>What the child does</th></tr><tr><td><strong>0–6 mo</strong></td><td><strong>BOA</strong> — Behavioral Observation Audiometry</td><td>Examiner observes <strong>reflexive/behavioral change</strong> to sound</td></tr><tr><td><strong>6 mo–2.5 yr</strong></td><td><strong>VRA</strong> — Visual Reinforcement Audiometry</td><td><strong>Turns toward sound</strong> → rewarded with visual stimulus</td></tr><tr><td><strong>2.5–5 yr</strong></td><td><strong>CPA</strong> — Conditioned Play Audiometry</td><td><strong>Performs a game task</strong> when sound is heard</td></tr><tr><td><strong>≥5 yr</strong></td><td><strong>Conventional audiometry</strong></td><td><strong>Raises hand/pushes button</strong> when sound is heard</td></tr></tbody></table>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "ufv>U[d9e#",
  "id": 1788347833375,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::AssessmentofAudiologicFunctionHearingFunction"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361375
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br/><br/>The {{c1::lateral process of the malleus}} produces the malleolar prominence and marks the inferior boundary of the pars flaccida."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Projects laterally from the upper manubrium. Anterior + posterior malleolar folds extend from this region. Pars flaccida / Prussak's space lie superior to it. Useful otoscopic and cholesteatoma landmark.<br><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "BGKzR[X3Jc",
  "id": 1788349361375,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361377
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br/><br/>The {{c1::manubrium (handle) of the malleus}} is embedded in the TM and terminates inferiorly at the {{c2::umbo}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "On otoscopy, the visible malleus can be followed: Lateral process → manubrium → umbo. The manubrium provides mechanical coupling between the TM and ossicular chain.<br/><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "kbDCOtpvbv",
  "id": 1788349361377,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361378
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br/><br/>The {{c1::head of the malleus}} lies in the epitympanum and articulates with the {{c2::body of the incus}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Together they form the incudomalleolar joint. The malleus head and incus body make up much of the ossicular content of the attic.<br><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "AzM%ZsWl+d",
  "id": 1788349361378,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361379
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br><br>The {{c1::tensor tympani}} inserts onto the {{c2::malleus near the upper manubrium/neck}} anteriorly."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Tensor tympani → malleus. Stapedius → stapes. Tensor tympani travels through the semicanal for tensor tympani and turns around the cochleariform process before inserting on the malleus.<br><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "c{&G-yG.0:",
  "id": 1788349361379,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361380
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br><br>The {{c1::short}}&nbsp;process of the incus points {{c2::posteriorly}},&nbsp;"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "while the long process projects inferiorly toward the stapes.<br>Incus orientation: Body = epitympanum; Short process = posterior; Long process = inferior; Lenticular process = distal tip toward stapes.<br><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "QJ<_/5/+A",
  "id": 1788349361380,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361381
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br><br>The short process of the incus sits in the {{c1::fossa incudis::specific}} and is stabilized by the {{c2::posterior incudal ligament}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "High-yield surgical landmark. Short process → posterior → fossa incudis. The fossa incudis is on the posterior epitympanic wall and helps orient you during mastoid/attic surgery.<br><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "Do>Bw{xOZC",
  "id": 1788349361381,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361382
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br/><br/>The portion of the ossicular chain particularly prone to erosion from chronic otitis/cholesteatoma is the {{c1::long process of the incus}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "A common operative finding is: Malleus intact → long process of incus eroded → stapes remains. This vulnerability is related to its relatively tenuous blood supply and exposed position.<br/><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "A;I?ELR7@g",
  "id": 1788349361382,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361383
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br/><br/>The {{c1::lenticular process}} is located at the distal end of the long process of the incus and articulates with the {{c2::stapes head}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Chain: Incus long process → lenticular process → incudostapedial joint → stapes head. The lenticular process is a distinct anatomical structure, not simply another name for the end of the long process.<br><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "mRj}Se_qX%",
  "id": 1788349361383,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361384
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br/><br/>From lateral to medial, the stapes consists of the {{c1::head → neck → anterior/posterior crura → footplate}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Head → articulates with lenticular process; Neck → stapedius insertion; Anterior + posterior crura → surround obturator foramen; Footplate → oval window.<br><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "j=?e+KTG*~",
  "id": 1788349361384,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361385
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br/><br/>The stapedius tendon emerges from the {{c1::pyramidal eminence}} and inserts onto the {{c2::neck of the stapes}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Useful visual chain: Pyramidal eminence → stapedius tendon → stapes neck. This is an excellent orientation landmark in the posterior mesotympanum.<br><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "Do|snAN[J^",
  "id": 1788349361385,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361386
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br/><br/>The stapes has {{c1::anterior and posterior crura}}, which connect the head/neck region to the footplate and surround the {{c2::obturator foramen}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "They are asymmetric: Anterior crus → generally thinner/straighter; Posterior crus → generally thicker/more curved. This can help orient an isolated stapes.<br><img src=\"tmpi00nyi0c.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "p%erg#aEOG",
  "id": 1788349361386,
  "media": [
    "tmpi00nyi0c.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361387
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br/><br/>The stapes footplate occupies the {{c1::oval window}} and is attached to its margin by the {{c2::annular ligament}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "The medial surface of the footplate interfaces with the vestibule/perilymph. Important relationship: Tympanic facial nerve → immediately superior to the oval window/stapes.<br><img src=\"paste-d51f922cbaabb468a1eab6bacbbf46fb98daded4.jpg\"><br>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "Q+yGTg$v3,",
  "id": 1788349361387,
  "media": [
    "paste-d51f922cbaabb468a1eab6bacbbf46fb98daded4.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788349361388
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Ossicles<br><br>The ossicular chain runs: malleus {{c1::head}} → incus {{c1::body}} → incus {{c1::long process}} → {{c1::lenticular process}} → stapes {{c1::head}} → stapes {{c1::footplate}} → oval window."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Two joints: Incudomalleolar joint = malleus head ↔ incus body; Incudostapedial joint = lenticular process ↔ stapes head. Two muscles: Tensor tympani → malleus; Stapedius → stapes. Useful posterior landmarks: Short process of incus → fossa incudis; Stapedius tendon → pyramidal eminence.<br><img src=\"paste-d51f922cbaabb468a1eab6bacbbf46fb98daded4.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "r5iyB!H]Dl",
  "id": 1788349361388,
  "media": [
    "paste-d51f922cbaabb468a1eab6bacbbf46fb98daded4.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Ossicles"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788614636382
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "<blockquote><div>The air-bone gap in SSCD results from {{c1::decreased::increased/decreased}} air-conduction sensitivity + {{c2::increased::</div>increased/decreased}} bone-conduction sensitivity.</blockquote>\n<div></div>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div><strong>Why does a third window cause both?</strong></div>\n<div><strong>Air conduction:</strong><br>\nStapes motion normally creates a pressure difference across the cochlear partition. The dehiscence provides an additional low-impedance outlet for inner-ear fluid displacement → some acoustic energy is shunted toward the third window → less pressure difference across the cochlear partition → less basilar-membrane motion → worse AC thresholds.</div>\n<div><strong>Bone conduction:</strong><br>\nSkull vibration moves the otic capsule. The third window provides an additional compliant outlet for inner-ear fluid movement → greater differential fluid motion/pressure across the cochlear partition → greater basilar-membrane motion → better BC thresholds, sometimes &lt;0 dB HL.</div>\n<div><strong>Key point:</strong> the cochlear nerve is not more sensitive. The third window changes the <strong>mechanics upstream of hair-cell/cochlear-nerve activation</strong>.</div>\n<div><strong>Clinical consequence:</strong><br>\n↓ AC + ↑ BC → low-frequency ABG despite a normal middle ear.</div><br>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "c<WK<t5~mC",
  "id": 1788614636382,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::SuperiorSemicircularCanalDehiscenceSSCD"
  ]
}
```

```json
  "fields": [

      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Dx based on this audiogram<br>{{c1::Superior semicircular canal dehiscense}}<br><img src=\"paste-a8fafd7a3ab19bb2f4dcec23e7c5770766124e22.jpg\"><br>"
    },
```

### Note 1788615592388
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Dx based on this vemp<br>{{c1::Bilateral superior semicircular canal dehiscense}}<br><img src=\"paste-d09adbd85eea2879c02ea90ba7df2a75bbb29911.jpg\"><br>"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div><strong>How to read this cVEMP:</strong></div>\n<ul>\n<li>\nIdentify the reproducible <strong>P1–N1 waveform</strong> at each stimulus intensity.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Threshold = lowest stimulus intensity that still produces a reproducible P1–N1 response.</strong>\n</li>\n<li>\nLeft: response persists to <strong>~65 dB nHL</strong>\n</li>\n<li>\nRight: response persists to <strong>~75 dB nHL</strong>\n</li>\n<li>\nThese are <strong>abnormally low cVEMP thresholds</strong> → increased sound sensitivity of the vestibular system → <strong>third-window physiology</strong>.\n</li>\n</ul>\n<div><strong>Why in SSCD?</strong><br>\nThe dehiscence creates a low-impedance third window → sound produces greater vestibular fluid displacement → the otolith/VEMP pathway can be activated at lower sound intensities.</div>\n<div><strong>Key pattern:</strong><br>\n<strong>SSCD → ↓ cVEMP threshold + ↑ oVEMP amplitude</strong></div>\n<div><em>Exact abnormal thresholds depend on the lab/stimulus protocol.</em></div><br>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "fx(o0w0=-E",
  "id": 1788615592388,
  "media": [
    "paste-d09adbd85eea2879c02ea90ba7df2a75bbb29911.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::AssessmentofAudiologicFunctionHearingFunction",
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::SuperiorSemicircularCanalDehiscenceSSCD"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986040
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The {{c1::bony}} labyrinth contains {{c2::peri}}lymph"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "The membranous labyrinth is suspended within the bony labyrinth. Perilymph occupies the space between them.<br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\"><br><img src=\"paste-db81479e39aac1212ecf0fe70e1b983685157b62.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
```

### Note 1788616986042
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The {{c1::membranous}} labyrinth contains {{c2::endo}}lymph"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "The membranous labyrinth includes the cochlear duct, utricle, saccule, and semicircular ducts.<br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\"><br><img src=\"paste-db81479e39aac1212ecf0fe70e1b983685157b62.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "b)su0gow0$",
  "id": 1788616986042,
  "media": [
    "paste-db81479e39aac1212ecf0fe70e1b983685157b62.jpg",
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986043
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "In the cochlea the&nbsp; scala {{c1::vestibuli}} and scala {{c1::tympani}} contain {{c2::peri}}lymph"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "These are spaces of the bony labyrinth and communicate with one another at the **helicotrema**.<br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "d=#[n%b}0k",
  "id": 1788616986043,
  "media": [
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986044
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "In the cochlea the scala {{c1::media (cochlear duct)}} contains {{c2::endo}}lymph"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "The scala media is part of the **membranous labyrinth**.<br><br>Superior → inferior:<br><b>Scala vestibuli — perilymph</b><br><b>Scala media — endolymph</b><br><b>Scala tympani — perilymph</b><br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "x?L`LQs{$X",
  "id": 1788616986044,
  "media": [
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986045
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The scala vestibuli and scala tympani communicate at the {{c1::helicotrema}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "The helicotrema is located at the **cochlear apex** and connects the two perilymph-filled scalae.<br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "jJeA:aW-=a",
  "id": 1788616986045,
  "media": [
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986046
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The {{c1::helicotrema}} is located at the apex of the cochlea."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "It is where the **scala vestibuli and scala tympani communicate**.<br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "PKwDaHF3d6",
  "id": 1788616986046,
  "media": [
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986047
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The {{c1::oval}} window communicates with the scala {{c2::vestibuli}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "The **stapes footplate** occupies the oval window. Stapes displacement therefore introduces pressure/volume displacement into the perilymph of the scala vestibuli.<br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "CysK1f=2G^",
  "id": 1788616986047,
  "media": [
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986048
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The {{c1::round}} window communicates with the scala {{c2::tympani}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "The round-window membrane acts as the compliant pressure-release boundary of the normal inner ear.<br><br>Normal mechanical pathway:<br><b>Stapes → oval window → scala vestibuli → cochlear partition → scala tympani → round window</b><br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "nB/x?y1&`2",
  "id": 1788616986048,
  "media": [
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986049
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "{{c1::Reissner}} membrane separates the scala {{c2::vestibuli}} from the scala {{c2::media}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Therefore, it separates:<br><b>perilymph (scala vestibuli)</b> from <b>endolymph (scala media)</b>.<br><br>Also called the <b>vestibular membrane</b>.<br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "gr$CN_xqd_",
  "id": 1788616986049,
  "media": [
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986050
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The {{c1::basilar}} membrane separates the scala {{c2::media}} from the scala {{c2::tympani}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Therefore, it separates:<br><b>endolymph (scala media)</b> from <b>perilymph (scala tympani)</b>.<br><br>The <b>organ of Corti sits on the basilar membrane</b>.<br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "Kqet-_<;`y",
  "id": 1788616986050,
  "media": [
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986051
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The membranous labyrinth forms a connected {{c1::endo}}lymphatic system."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "The **semicircular ducts, utricle, saccule, and cochlear duct** are components of the membranous labyrinth and contain endolymph.<br><br><b>Membranous structure → endolymph inside<br></b>Boney structure → perilymph inside<br><img src=\"tmphr8saoa7.jpg\"><br><img src=\"tmppp9u__90.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "LE=:-y3wY{",
  "id": 1788616986051,
  "media": [
    "tmphr8saoa7.jpg",
    "tmppp9u__90.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788616986053
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Each bony semicircular {{c1::canal}} contains {{c2::peri}}lymph surrounding a membranous semicircular {{c1::duct}}, which contains {{c2::endo}}lymph"
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Don't use <b>canal</b> and <b>duct</b> interchangeably when thinking about the fluid anatomy.<br><br><b>Bony canal → perilymph → membranous duct → endolymph</b><br><br>In SSCD, the defect is in the <b>bone over the superior semicircular canal</b>.<br><img src=\"paste-db81479e39aac1212ecf0fe70e1b983685157b62.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "trPj&TO],6",
  "id": 1788616986053,
  "media": [
    "paste-db81479e39aac1212ecf0fe70e1b983685157b62.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788619299114
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Movement of the {{c1::basilar}} membrane creates shearing motion between the organ of Corti and tectorial membrane, resulting in stereocilia deflection."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Sound → pressure difference across cochlear partition → basilar membrane displacement → relative motion within the organ of Corti → stereocilia deflection.<br><br>This is the mechanical step that converts the cochlear traveling wave into hair-cell stimulation.<br><img src=\"tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "bK:AKo6S`2",
  "id": 1788619299114,
  "media": [
    "tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788619299115
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Deflection toward the {{c1::tallest}} stereocilia opens hair-cell mechanotransduction channels and causes depolarization."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Tip links connect adjacent stereocilia.<br><br>Deflection toward tallest → ↑ tip-link tension → MET channels open.<br>Deflection away → MET channels close → hyperpolarization.<br><img src=\"tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "x@RFRCK6%c",
  "id": 1788619299115,
  "media": [
    "tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788619299116
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Opening cochlear hair-cell mechanotransduction channels causes {{c1::K+::ion}} influx from endolymph, resulting in depolarization."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "This is unusual compared with conventional neurons.<br><br>Endolymph has <b>high K+</b> and an approximately <b>+80 mV endocochlear potential</b>, creating a large electrochemical driving force for K+ to enter the hair cell through its apical MET channels.<br><br>This is how <b>mechanical stereocilia deflection becomes an electrical receptor potential</b>.<br><img src=\"tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "F%(&~,fbCr",
  "id": 1788619299116,
  "media": [
    "tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788619299117
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Hair-cell depolarization opens {{c1::voltage-gated Ca²⁺}} channels, causing {{c2::glutamate::neurotransmitter}} release onto {{c3::spiral}} ganglion afferents."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Full sequence:<br><br><b>Stereocilia deflection → MET channels → K+ influx → depolarization → Ca²⁺ influx → glutamate → spiral ganglion → cochlear nerve</b><br><img src=\"tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "PQ7g}XHOeq",
  "id": 1788619299117,
  "media": [
    "tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788619299118
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The stria vascularis generates the approximately {{c1::+80}} &nbsp;mV endocochlear potential."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "The stria vascularis maintains the unusual ionic/electrical environment of scala-media endolymph.<br><br>Together, <b>high endolymphatic K+ + positive endocochlear potential</b> provide the electrochemical driving force for K+ influx during hair-cell mechanotransduction.<br><br>Think of it as the <b>battery powering cochlear transduction</b>.<br><img src=\"tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "y`;eI@n1:=",
  "id": 1788619299118,
  "media": [
    "tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788619299119
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Outer hair-cell electromotility is mediated by {{c1::prestin::protein}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Changes in OHC membrane potential cause prestin-mediated changes in cell length.<br><br>This feeds mechanical energy back into the organ of Corti/basilar membrane—the <b>cochlear amplifier</b>.<br><br>Consequences:<br>• increased sensitivity to quiet sounds<br>• sharper frequency tuning<br>• nonlinear compression<br><img src=\"tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "ous*Uht*j!",
  "id": 1788619299119,
  "media": [
    "tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788619299120
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Otoacoustic emissions primarily assess {{c1::outer}} hair-cell function."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "OHC electromotility generates mechanical energy that can propagate backward:<br><br><b>cochlea → ossicular chain/TM → ear canal</b><br><br>It can then be detected with a microphone as an OAE.<br><br>Therefore <b>present OAEs indicate functioning OHC/cochlear-amplifier mechanics</b>, not necessarily intact neural transmission.<br><img src=\"tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "kLztH%3{.6",
  "id": 1788619299120,
  "media": [
    "tmpjiv7hnhw.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788701403147
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "{{c1::Deiters}} cells aka outer phalangeal cells, support the {{c2::outer}} hair cells and contribute to the reticular lamina via their phalangeal processes."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "There are typically ~3 rows, corresponding to the 3 rows of outer hair cells. Deiters cells support the base of OHCs, and their long phalangeal processes extend apically to form part of the structural lattice of the reticular lamina.<br><img src=\"tmp1glm187v.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "v1mPnjj2BD",
  "id": 1788701403147,
  "media": [
    "tmp1glm187v.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788701403152
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The inner and outer {{c1::pillar}} cells form the {{c2::tunnel of Corti}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Pillar cells form a rigid structural framework between the inner and outer hair-cell regions.<br><img src=\"tmp1glm187v.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "z(c`g{>JqO",
  "id": 1788701403152,
  "media": [
    "tmp1glm187v.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788701403154
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "From medial → lateral, the major supporting structures/cells of the organ of Corti are: inner hair cell → {{c1::pillar cells}} → outer hair cells + {{c2::Deiters cells}} → {{c3::Hensen cells}} → {{c4::Claudius cells}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Inner phalangeal cells support IHCs. Deiters cells support OHCs. Pillar cells form the tunnel of Corti. Hensen and Claudius cells form the progressively more lateral supporting epithelium. Boettcher cells lie beneath Claudius cells, especially in the basal turn; recognize the name, but this is relatively low-yield.<br><br><img src=\"tmp1glm187v.jpg\">"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "A{OBc8l;Pi",
  "id": 1788701403154,
  "media": [
    "tmp1glm187v.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788702666914
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The cochlear duct (scala media) communicates with the {{c1::saccule}} via the {{c2::ductus reuniens}}, thereby connecting cochlear endolymph to the endolymphatic duct/sac system."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Cochlear duct → ductus reuniens → saccule → saccular duct → endolymphatic duct → endolymphatic sac<br><div>The utricle similarly communicates with the endolymphatic duct via the <strong>utricular duct</strong>.</div>\n<div>The endolymphatic duct/sac participates in <strong>endolymph volume and ionic homeostasis</strong>; this is not simply passive bulk “drainage.”</div><img alt=\"Ductus reuniens - Wikipedia\" src=\"gray924.png\"><br>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "xrt,nMS]+a",
  "id": 1788702666914,
  "media": [
    "gray924.png"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::Anatomy::Cochlea"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788716176976
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Preferred surgical management of symptomatic SSCD is {{c1::superior semicircular canal plugging::procedure}}, performed through either a {{c2::middle cranial fossa or transmastoid::2}} approach."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "<div><div><strong>Goal:</strong> eliminate the pathologic <strong>third-window physiology</strong>.</div>\n<div><strong>Plugging</strong></div>\n<ul>\n<li>\nOccludes the superior canal on either side of/around the dehiscence.\n</li>\n<li>\nPrevents abnormal fluid motion through the affected canal.\n</li>\n<li>\nMost commonly used strategy for durable third-window elimination.\n</li>\n<li>\nTradeoff: intentionally reduces function of that superior canal → postoperative vestibular hypofunction/disequilibrium can occur.\n</li>\n</ul>\n<div><strong>Resurfacing/capping</strong></div>\n<ul>\n<li>\nAttempts to restore a rigid boundary over the dehiscence while preserving canal patency/function.\n</li>\n<li>\nAttractive theoretically because it may preserve superior-canal function.\n</li>\n<li>\nHowever, resurfacing alone has historically had concern for persistent/recurrent third-window physiology; evidence comparing techniques is heterogeneous. \n</li>\n</ul>\n<div><strong>Approach ≠ repair technique</strong></div>\n<ul>\n<li>\n<strong>MCF</strong> = direct superior visualization; requires craniotomy.\n</li>\n<li>\n<strong>Transmastoid</strong> = avoids craniotomy; feasibility depends on anatomy and gives different access/visualization.\n</li>\n<li>\nEither can be used for canal occlusion/repair; recent evidence continues to support both as viable approaches rather than one universally superior route.&nbsp;</li></ul></div><div><a href=\"https://otosurgeryatlas.stanford.edu/otologic-surgery-atlas/vestibular-surgery/superior-semicircular-canal-dehiscence/#\">https://otosurgeryatlas.stanford.edu/otologic-surgery-atlas/vestibular-surgery/superior-semicircular-canal-dehiscence/#</a></div><div><br></div><div><img src=\"paste-99b309d8d0b7e0426161c6705e3a4a1a9c6fdf58.jpg\"></div><div><img src=\"paste-5643bad4c4bc32740a17c717bb85ed1af0d31f04.jpg\"></div>"
    }
  ],
  "guid": "rbDrtdYOXb",
  "id": 1788716176976,
  "media": [
    "paste-5643bad4c4bc32740a17c717bb85ed1af0d31f04.jpg",
    "paste-99b309d8d0b7e0426161c6705e3a4a1a9c6fdf58.jpg"
  ],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::SuperiorSemicircularCanalDehiscenceSSCD"
  ]
}
```

```json
{
  "fields": [
```

```json
      "value": "What CT T bone reformat is this?<br>{{c1::Poschl}}<br><img src=\"paste-2153103a0bec4440dc1d33fa841198a1eb61820e.jpg\"><br>"
```

```json
  "fields": [
```

```json
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "Dupilumab treats CRSwNP by blocking {{c1::IL-4Rα}}, thereby inhibiting {{c2::IL-4 and IL-13}} signaling."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Often particularly attractive when CRSwNP coexists with asthma or atopic dermatitis, or when smell loss is prominent.<br><img src=\"paste-168dac800f722b698b2ce23f1f49977661ac4a1a.jpg\">"
    }
```

```json
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788732559842
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "The revised 60/60 rule recommends referral for cochlear implant evaluation when an ear has a PTA {{c1::≥60 dB HL}} and unaided word recognition score {{c1::≤60%}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "This is a referral guideline, not a strict candidacy criterion. Each ear is considered independently. Patients outside these cutoffs may still warrant CI evaluation if appropriately fitted hearing aids provide inadequate benefit."
    }
  ],
  "guid": "HpjJ_BIwN%",
  "id": 1788732559842,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::CochlearImplantsCI"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788732559843
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "{{c1::Word recognition score (WRS)}} assesses open-set monosyllabic word recognition presented {{c2::through headphones/inserts at suprathreshold levels}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "WRS is part of conventional unaided audiometry. It assesses how well an ear can recognize speech when the signal is made audible.<br><br>Do not confuse WRS with aided speech testing used during formal CI evaluation."
    }
  ],
  "guid": "onLU3ynI-y",
  "id": 1788732559843,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::CochlearImplantsCI"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788732559844
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "{{c1::Consonant-Nucleus-Consonant (CNC)}} testing assesses {{c2::aided open-set monosyllabic word recognition in an aided condition}}."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "CNC words are presented with an appropriately fitted hearing aid during CI evaluation.<br><br>Because isolated monosyllabic words provide little linguistic context, CNC testing is a relatively demanding measure of the speech information available through amplification.<br><br>An aided CNC ≤50% in the ear being considered for implantation supports CI candidacy under ACI Alliance recommendations."
    }
  ],
  "guid": "Hv-O<~1(~`",
  "id": 1788732559844,
  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
  "tags": [
    "Crabs::Residency::Otology::CochlearImplantsCI"
  ]
}
```

### Note 1788732559845
```json
{
  "fields": [
    {
      "name": "Text",
      "value": "{{c1::AzBio}} assesses {{c2::aided open-set sentence recognition}} spoken by multiple talkers."
    },
    {
      "name": "Extra",
      "value": "Scoring is based on the percentage of words correctly repeated. Testing can be performed in quiet or noise.<br><br>Sentence context can help the listener reconstruct degraded auditory information, so sentence performance can be substantially better than isolated-word performance."
    }
  ],
  "guid": "qxF[3>bl:{",

  "media": [],
  "model_id": 1524000174356,
```
